# Roadmap

The official list of what is built, what is being built, and what comes
next, so anyone can follow along and pick something up. **Every pull
request updates this file**: its item's status, and a line in the
[update log](#update-log) at the bottom.

The design behind each item lives in the linked doc; this page is the
index and the status board, not the spec.

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| ✅ | Built and merged |
| 🚧 | In progress: partly built and merged, or on a branch or open PR |
| ⏭️ | Next up |
| 📋 | Planned |

**Want to help?** Pick a 📋 item, read its design doc, and open an issue
or a draft PR saying you're on it, so work isn't doubled. Each item is
one PR (`CLAUDE.md` has the workflow: tests, screenshots, one feature per
PR).

## At a glance (2026-11-22)

**335 table items built, 10 in progress, 13 planned** (plus `6` closed/parked spikes/steps: `TS5`, `TS6`, `TS7`, `TS8`, `E5b`, and `ES1b` parked on the licence call; recounted from the status columns below on 2026-11-22 after the `#739`–`#746` batch, `VFX1`, `ES2`, `ES4`, `ES4b`, `ES5a`, `ES2c`, `ES4c`, `ES4d`, `ES5b` and `ES5c`, excluding the legend). With `DB5` (`#725`), Baked Asset Store `B1`–`B4` (`#726`), and live modular collapse + universal gravity (`DE3`, `DE4c`, `DE5`, `#727`) merged alongside `PATCH01`–`PATCH05`, `CF6`, `DE1`–`DE2`, `WL2`, `GH1`–`H4`, and the documentation rewrite (`DOC3`), `3` table items remain in progress (`DE4` Linux `valgrind:perf` gate, `CL0` Cutscene Lane `C1`–`C4`, and `U15` the client UI style kit), section [1h](#1h-editor-ui-2026-rebuild-from-the-aether-mockups) adds the editor UI rebuild (`EU4`, `EU5`, `EU10` built; `EU1`-`EU3`, `EU6`-`EU9` in progress), section [1i](#1i-editor-build-out-the-tools-a-scene-building-editor-needs) the editor build-out (`EB11`, `EB1`, `EB2`, `EB4`, `EB4b`, `EB9` built; the rest planned) and section [1g2](#1g2-tick-timing-accuracy) the `PERF-TT1` follow-up, and section [7b](#7b-engine-core--game-ip-split)'s engine core / game IP split carries the `7` planned ones (`ES2b`, `ES5`–`ES9`, and `DEP2` in [7c](#7c-dependency-and-licence-audit)) behind `ES0` (✅), `ES1a` (✅), `ES2` (✅), `ES3` (✅), `ES4` (✅), `ES4b` (✅), `ES4c` (✅), `ES4d` (✅), `ES5a` (✅), `ES5b` (✅) and `ES5c` (✅). `npm run lint` (the SAB no-console guard) passes with zero findings. The game is playable end to end (`npm start`), and the open `🚧` items and follow-ups below remain:

What is left, by area (from the tables and active engineering backlog):

- **Destruction closeout (`DE4` perf gate & `DE3b` heuristic)**: Live client visual collapse (`DE5`), universal gravity + full collider cleanup (`DE4`), support graph (`DE3`), persistence (`DE4b`), and middle hit-testing (`DE4c`) are verified in headless Chromium (`144/144` upper parts collapsed to ground); only the Linux/WSL `valgrind:perf` baseline (`DE4`) and post-`DE3b` `closed` envelope heuristic re-tune remain open.
- **Cutscene Lane (`C1`–`C4` after `C0`/`MJ1`)**: Cutscene schemas/validator (`C0`) are landed (`🚧` `CL0`), while MuJoCo offline bake, playback runtime, and gated WASM lane remain (`C1`–`C4`).
- **Fan-out at 1,000 players & sending**: One region finishes ~43% of ticks at 1,000 players (`PF2` follow-up: native `interestEntries`), no 1,000-session Edge run has been measured, the `MMO_UWS_CORK` send-batching gate is unrun, and real-hardware `uWS` benchmarks + vendored `.node` binary packaging remain pending.
- **Player-facing UI & Wildlife (`WL1`/`WL2` follow-ups)**: Client lacks UI windows for player-to-player trade (`0x21`), auction house (`0x21`), and crafting (`EA27`); birds, snakes, sea giants, flying predator, and megafauna boss brain/UI remain follow-ups (`WL2` editor `RigSummaryPanel` is now built in `apps/editor/src/ui/rig-preview.tsx`).
- **Physics & Rendering follow-ups**: Full `CharacterVirtual` slopes/steps wiring, dungeon physics re-bake, GPU cel-shading stills (noon/dusk/night) + outline cost sweep ([`CEL-SHADING-AUDIT.md`](internals/CEL-SHADING-AUDIT.md)), `terrain-mask.bin` serving + `path` channel ordering ([`TERRAIN-MASK.md`](internals/TERRAIN-MASK.md)), spline inter-town road rendering (`SG2b`/`SG11`), and `WX1` weather wet-look/ripples/audio couplings.
- **Branches & CI / Windows test cleanup**: Unmerged PRs/branches (`#699` `TF1`, `fix/fx-budgets-schema`, `feat/animal-anatomy-rig`), plus pre-existing suite failures outside this branch (`world-server` `accounts`/`assignment`, `editor` `sidecar-manager`, `build` `deploy`, `engine-core` `uWS` on Windows Node 25 / WAL cleanup / `world-migration`, `addon-terrain-kernel` pretest `bash`, and POSIX script tests).

## Where things live

- **The game client (`apps/client`) is what players see**, and only that:
  the world, and the player's own interface (health and resource bars,
  chat, parties, quests, inventory, character sheet, settings).
- **The editor (`apps/editor`) is where every developer tool lives.**
  Its play mode runs the game against a local server and is the engine's
  debugger: performance graphs, draw calls and triangles, level-of-detail
  views, network traffic, wireframe, effect showcases, entity
  inspection, navmesh and path overlays.

`npm run client` starts the player's client, `npm run dev` the
development environment (the server and editor), and `npm run editor`
the editor alone.

Developer tooling is mounted only in the editor's play mode.

---

## 1b. Admin console: control the running game

The development client's (`npm run dev`) control over the live world, to
test every part of the engine: then with the rest of the developer tools
in the editor's play mode (E4). Every change goes through the server,
which accepts admin commands only from admin accounts or a development
server, and logs them. Source definitions (prefabs, species, rules files)
stay read-only here; this edits the running game.

| # | Item | Status | Needs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | **Admin channel**: an admin command message, admin accounts (and every account on a development server), a command log | ✅ | 0x10 `help`/`whoami` live; `MMO_ADMINS` + `MMO_DEV_SERVER=1`; A2+ adds world-editing commands; the admin console is also in the editor's play-mode debugger (#111) |
| A2 | Inspect and edit any entity: pick or search it, every component and field, change any value | ✅ | `inspect`, `find` (wire id, component or creature name), and `set` across every registered component: numeric fields, Needs, stat bases (`Stat`), Appearance.look, Chunk.lod. Tags use god, noclip, addcomponent and removecomponent. Picking in the editor's game view is E6. By design, not writable: the chunk id and seed, and Appearance parts (the A6 `appearance` command). Tests: `test/admin-find-set.test.ts`, `test/net/gateway-admin.test.ts` (#531) |
| A3 | Spawn anything at the cursor or the player: species, archetypes, props, PICG assets; despawn | ✅ | `spawnlist`/`spawn` (archetypes and creature kinds, at the avatar or x/z) and `despawn` live; a despawn button on the selection card; cursor targeting in the play view (`ground-pick.ts`, #538, E4). Creature kinds via `CreatureSystem.kinds()` (#534) |
| A4 | The player: health, mana, stamina and any stat; teleport; fly and no-clip; invulnerable; time of day and weather | ✅ | `teleport` (ground snap), `heal`, `fly` bit live; stats via `set`; `god` toggles the God tag (no combat/hunger/drowning/trap Health loss) and peaks every skill, kept when switched off; `noclip` toggles the Noclip tag (free vertical flight, dungeon walls let go); `time` shows and sets the clock (newcomers via welcome, live clients by broadcast) and `weather` forces the sky (auto/clear/rain/snow/ash, same path); `injure {part, kind?, amount?}` (#99) and `skill {id, value}` (#112) live; `heal` also fills mana and makes the body whole |
| A5 | Loot and inventory: browse the loot pools, put items in the inventory, grant gold | ✅ | `items` (browse the pool), `give` (into the bag), `drop` (on the ground), `loot` (mob drops round you) live; `gold` grants gold (Live ops' avatar card has a Gold button); the creature grid edits `drops.gold` |
| A6 | Appearance and equipment editor, live on the character | ✅ | Merged (PR #191): `appearance` admin command (parts/colours per slot, validated against the catalog, live via sim.setLook). Equipment overlays re-apply. Open: the editor panel. |
| A7 | **Bots in web workers**: an admin command spawns N simulated players (each its own socket, moving and fighting) in worker threads, to stress the server and feel play under load | ✅ | U14 (worker pool), A1; builds on `scripts/world-stress` |

| EA32 | Wire-ready editor prototype and whole-game save package (W1–W5) | ✅ | Packaged under `docs/library/mmo-engine-editor-ui/`: the single-file prototype now routes edits through NexusWire's serializable command bus, persists the whole GameProject locally, writes drafts / versions / commands through HTTP, reconciles by `manifest.savedAt`, adds objects to the selected scene, and carries GSAP-driven standard / scene-first layouts. `backend-reference/` contains the executable endpoint contract and zero-dependency conformance server. This is not the production editor: the Express backend implementation and React consumption of the document remain separate work |

## 1d. Editor interaction repairs

An audit of the modular editor shell found 23 interactions that are dead
or contradict the design doc (toolbar tools not governing viewports,
Save/Undo/Redo and Pause/Step/Stop without handlers, advertised shortcuts
that reload the browser, play-menu choices that don't reach the server, no
play tint or read-only gate, selections that never reach Details, panels
with no retry). Fixing them as one wave, tracked here.

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R1 | Toolbar and play cluster do what they say (active tool governs every viewport; Save/Undo/Redo, Pause/Step/Stop, play-menu setup, play tint + read-only Details) | ✅ | `fix/ed-toolbar-play` + `fix/ed-panels` gating; Pause/Step render disabled with reasons (no server control exists); preview-only play dimensions badged |
| R2 | Shortcuts and menus are honest (implemented with focus guards, or no hint) | ✅ | `fix/ed-keys-menus`: F5/Shift+F5/V/W/E/R wired; staged rows disabled with no kbd hint; drag-to-place removed until the scene viewport slice lands |
| R3 | Panels behave (sculpt gated, background pan, retry, drag thresholds, validated prompts) | ✅ | `fix/ed-panels` |
| R4 | Selection and deep links hold (publish picks, hash round-trip, scope Details, clean disconnect) | ✅ | `fix/ed-selection`; panel highlight restoration on reload deferred |
| R5 | Pause and Step drive the owned server's tick loop | ✅ | `feat/editor-pause-step`: admin `pause`/`resume`/`step` on the gateway (`TickLoop.step`/`rearm`); the play cluster's Pause toggles Resume, Step runs one tick while paused. Server-side pause only. |

authoring, and give the client a locomotion-blending layer. Each phase
ends with a gate and the next one does not start until it is green;

Every tsconfig is `incremental`. TypeScript-only build on Windows: 1521 s and 197 `tsc` runs before, 131 s and 27 after (117 s warm). |

Revisit only if the client migrates to WebGPU, after checking live demo FPS/budget, cel-style fit, and commercial licence terms; `stylized-components` (MIT) and terrain detail layers remain our active ground-cover path. |

one propagates to every panel: audit in the project library
(`editor-dry-audit/DRY-AUDIT.md`).

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | One `NodeGraph<N, E>` canvas for the PICG, flow and story graphs (pan, zoom, fit, drag, select) | ✅ | #199 |
| M2 | One HTTP layer (`requestJson`, `callEnvelope`, `IssuesError`, `refuseInvalid`) for every client and host route | ✅ | #200; `callEnvelope` reaches the graph, dungeon, HUD, pipeline and terrain panels with #207 |
| M3 | `DocBar` and `useDocActions` for every document editor's Save, Undo, Redo, Revert | ✅ | #201 |

| M7 | `useDocument` (draft, saved copy, dirty by value, undo and redo) for flow, story and dungeon | ✅ | #207, in main with #209; flow gained Undo and Redo |

Lifecycle controller and hook (`ui/document-lifecycle.ts`, `ui/use-document-lifecycle.ts`): new, load, import, export, retry. Tests: `test/document-lifecycle.test.ts` (#535). Panels adopt the hook as they move onto it |

## 1g2. Tick timing accuracy

| ID | Item | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PERF-TT1 | Window the per-system figures in `TickTimings` the way tick durations already are | 📋 | Found while building the editor profiler (`EU7`). `endTick` writes a 1,000-tick ring, so `p50`/`p99` are a window; `recordSystem` keeps running `sum`/`max`/`n` per system since the process started, so a system's `avg` is diluted by all history and its `max` never decays after one spike — yet `SystemCost.max` is documented as "Max milliseconds in the window". A regression that starts now is invisible. Fix is a ring per system (33 systems x 1,000 x 8 B is about 264 KB) or an explicitly-named decaying statistic; either way it is a change to the tick path, so it needs its own PR and a `valgrind:perf` run. Until then the editor's profiler labels which number comes from which window |

## 1h. Editor UI 2026 (rebuild from the Aether mockups)

Doc: [`EDITOR-UI-2026.md`](EDITOR-UI-2026.md). The owner sent two
mockups on 2026-11-22: one for what the editor should do, one for how it
should look. The design note maps both onto today's editor screen by
screen and splits the work into the slices below. `EU1`-`EU5` are
presentation over data the editor already holds; `EU6` onward is new
capability, one PR each with captures.

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EU1 | Design-language layer: `forge.css` tokens, chrome metrics and type scale, remapping the existing `--m3-*` roles so every panel restyles at once | 🚧 | Built on `claude/project-thread-iob1l5`. Opt-in (`?theme=forge`, or the stored choice in `theme.ts`); the Material 3 scheme stays the default so the two can be compared. 126 hardcoded hex values across 21 files are the known cleanup list |
| EU2 | Shell chrome: breadcrumb and context chips (tool, branch, server, problems) | 🚧 | Built on `claude/project-thread-iob1l5` as `ui/forge-chrome.tsx`. Every value is state the shell already holds: the shared tool store, `editorSelection`, `playUiStore`, `/api/editor/vcs/status`, and the shell's own problem total. Forge-only — no Material 3 counterpart to restyle. The play transport stays on the toolbar |
| EU3 | Inspector rebuild: Details / Components / Systems tabs, liveness read-out, component rows with letter badges and field summaries, archetype card | 🚧 | Built on `claude/project-thread-iob1l5` over the same `useEcsInspectorSession()` hook the Material 3 panel mounts, so behaviour cannot drift. Field types and per-component system access come from the committed ECS graph |
| EU4 | Outliner badges; systems list with counts and signatures | ✅ | Both halves built. `ui/forge-systems.tsx` inverts `/api/editor/graph`'s component-keyed access into one row per system — writes, reads, excludes, weakest confidence on the row — joined to live per-system tick cost through the profiler's shared poll (38 systems, 27 declaring). `ui/outliner-badges.ts` + `-view.tsx` put the component chips and the `nC` count on every Scenes Outliner row; the ECS entity list had badged its rows all along, this tree simply never did |
| EU5 | Dock regrouped to four tabs; Source Control moves in | ✅ | **Integrations** built (`ui/forge-integrations.tsx`): one status list over the runtime node, the git worktree, the advisory soft locks the reference calls Conflict Guard, and whether the committed ECS graph still matches HEAD — plus VS Code and Blender listed as *not set up* with the slice that would build each. The regrouping landed once `EU8` and `EU9` gave two of the four tabs something to hold: under forge the bottom dock is **Console · Problems · Source Control · Integrations** (`ui/dock-tabs.ts`), Material 3 keeps Output / Problems / Bakes, and Output and Bakes fold into the console, which already carries both streams. Two departures from the reference, both deliberate: Problems stays (the reference has no validator over the content on disk; ours finds real breakage), and Code is not a tab (`EU9`'s answer is a handoff, which belongs on the rows that know a `file:line`). A layout saved under one theme opens the other's counterpart, never an empty pane |
| EU6 | Viewport gizmos, axis widget, and a Scene / Game / Prefab mode segment | 🚧 | Gizmos were already there (`TransformControls` in `ui/scene-view.ts`) but on the viewport's own local state; they now follow the shared tool store, so the toolbar, the context bar and the gizmo on screen cannot disagree, and `select` puts the gizmo away. Corner axis triad added (three's `ViewHelper`), a viewport flag like grid and gizmos. The Scene / Game / Prefab segment is still open |
| EU7 | Profiler panel; per-system tick cost | 🚧 | The feed was already there: `TickTimings` in engine-core records both, the debug API serves them (`query: "timings"`), and the Debug workspace listed the top five. `ui/forge-profiler.tsx` adds the budget — every system as a share of the 33.33 ms tick, p50/p99 against it, headroom at p99 — over the SAME poll, now shared by both presentations. Open: a tick-duration sparkline (the ring is server-side; only percentiles cross the wire) |
| EU8 | Console, and runtime discovery (probe local ports, list, attach) | 🚧 | **Console** built (`ui/console-log.ts` + `ui/forge-console.tsx`): one bounded ring of 1,000 entries over the three streams the editor already produced — the play server's log, bakes and the editor's own notifications, which until now flashed for 3.6 s and were gone. Filters by source, level and text; server lines take their level from the same `lineTone` the Server tab colours by. It polls nothing of its own. Open: runtime discovery (probe local ports, list, attach) — the attach half exists (`server-gate.ts`, `mirror-connect.ts`) |
| EU9 | VS Code handoff rather than an embedded IDE | 🚧 | Built: `code-handoff.ts` (pure — parses the graph's `file:line`, the refusals, the `vscode://` URI, the copyable `code -g` command), `code-host.ts` (`GET /api/editor/code` probes PATH, `POST /api/editor/code/open` spawns `code -g` with an argv array, never a shell string), and `ui/open-in-code.tsx`, which every systems row carries. Repository-relative paths only, nothing under `assets/` — `vcs-host.ts`'s refusal, restated because this is the second tool that could cross it. Deliberate, and the reason stands: `files-host.ts` serves whitelisted document kinds only, so an editor over arbitrary source and a terminal would undo both guards. Open: the four-tab regrouping the Code tab belongs to |
| EU10 | Blender live-link: bridge add-on, glTF import | ✅ | Built. `scripts/blender/mmo_live_link.py` is the add-on to copy — Blender's own Python and the standard library, nothing to `pip install` — which exports the selection to glTF and POSTs it to `/api/editor/blender/push`; the host streams it into `assets/live/`, gitignored in full and refused by `vcs-host.ts`, so a work in progress cannot be committed by accident. `ui/blender-panel.tsx` is the receiving end, with the editor's own `ModelPreview` on the newest model. **HTTP, not a websocket** (a socket would only buy editor-to-Blender commands and needs a modal timer in Blender's main thread to pump). Guide: [BLENDER-LINK.md](BLENDER-LINK.md) |

| EG13 | Persistence cadence and query limits as server settings, in the editor | ✅ | Audit row 12. Save interval, snapshots kept, WAL limit, query rows and timeout. Merged in #304 |
| EG14 | World and time settings in the editor (day length and offset, bots), env still overrides | ✅ | Audit row 13. Seed, chunk size and radius are fixed by the bake and shown read-only. Merged in #305 |

## 1i. Editor build-out (the tools a scene-building editor needs)

The phase after the 2026 UI rebuild: make the editor one you can build a
game *in*, not only inspect one with. The list was derived from the code
rather than from a wish list, and it found what the mockup walk found —
almost everything is present as capability, and the gaps are **joins**.
The editor already loads a GLB, resolves an `AssetRef` to a URL, draws
terrain chunks, drives a transform gizmo and undoes a command; the scene
viewport still draws a grey box per node. Sizes below are rough.

| ID | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| EB1 | Model-accurate scene viewport | ✅ | `scene-visuals.ts` (pure) and `scene-visuals-host.ts` build the size the engine actually spawns each placeable at — a creature's capsule is `CHARACTER_CAPSULE` times the body scale `CreatureService` resolves, a prop's stand-in moves with `content/scale.json` — and `GET /api/editor/scene/visuals` hands the browser the table with a model URL per id. `ui/scene-view.ts` keeps a per-URL model cache, draws the pack's GLB at the server's scale where there is one, and the sized capsule or box where there is not. Markers stay for spawn points and groups, which have no size to be accurate about |
| EB2 | Multi-select in the scene | ✅ | `scene-selection.ts` holds the set and the click rules (plain replaces, ctrl or cmd toggles, shift ranges over the rows the outliner shows), `scene-align.ts` the arithmetic of align and distribute, `ui/align-bar.tsx` their controls. The outliner and the viewport both pick into it, the whole set lights up while the gizmo rides the primary, a drag moves every selected node by the same delta in one undo step, and Delete takes the set. The shared `ui/editor-selection.ts` still carries one node, because that is what the Details pane shows: the set is the scene workspace's. Box-select is not in: it needs a drag that does not orbit, which is a viewport-input change of its own |
| EB3 | Authored prefabs | 📋 | A `prefab` today is a reference string to a species, prop or PICG id (`packages/scene/src/place.ts`). Composing nodes into a reusable prefab, instancing it and overriding one instance is a new document type plus instancing rules. The one genuinely large piece. **Held until the owner has reviewed `#751`** |
| EB4 | Viewport-first Scenes layout | ✅ | The canvas is the workspace: the viewport's controls float on it instead of pushing it down the page, and the placeables moved out from under it into `ui/scene-palette.tsx`, a searchable grouped column beside the Outliner (`placeables-filter.ts` holds the search, `ui/placeables-store.ts` is the one hook both it and the viewport read). A click places at the camera's ground point, a drag still carries `mmo-placeable:<id>` |
| EB4b | The forge theme reaches the Scenes controls | ✅ | The cause was one layer deeper than the row assumed, and so is the fix: every primitive in `ui/components.tsx` reads the legacy shadcn aliases (`--primary`, `--background`, `--input`), which `forge.css` never redefined, so every Button, Input and Select in the editor stayed Material 3 purple. The forge block now remaps all twelve, each in the channel space `app.css` reads it in, and `test/forge-aliases.test.ts` fails if a later alias is added to one theme and not the other |
| EB5 | Frontend screen author (login, loading, character select, character creation) | 📋 | The screens are React components in `apps/client/src/ui/`; nothing authors their layout. The in-game HUD does have a drag editor (`ui/hud-editor-logic.ts` over `content/ui/hud.json`) with anchor maths and overlap detection — the shape to extend, drawn with the client style kit (`U15`). **Held until the owner has reviewed `#751`** |
| EB6 | Flow preview in place | 📋 | "Play from" opens the client in another tab, so the flow graph never shows the screen. The client already takes `?start=<node>` |
| EB7 | Character creation rules | 📋 | `ui/AppearanceEditor.tsx` edits one character's appearance through the server API; what a player may choose at creation lives in `content/species` and the rules files, and no panel owns it |
| EB8 | New module scaffold | 📋 | Every one of the 35 modules was hand-written. A generator for `module.json`, a server entry, an editor entry and the registration |
| EB9 | Blast radius before disabling a module | ✅ | `module-blast.ts` walks `dependsOn` transitively over the panel's own state: every enabled module that goes too, the systems leaving the tick order, the editor panels that disappear, the sidecars that stop. Each enabled card carries the one-line cost; a switch that takes other modules with it asks first (`ui/blast-dialog.tsx`). It also fixed a real bug — the panel closed the set one level deep, so turning off `economy` left `civic` behind and the host refused the save |
| EB10 | Module settings in the editor | 📋 | `#749` put per-module settings in `game.json`; the Modules panel edits enablement only |
| EB11 | Name the editor's layers, and test them | ✅ | The seam existed and was unstated: hosts validate and write, clients carry one route family, stores hold shared state, views draw. [`EDITOR.md`](EDITOR.md) now says so and `apps/editor/test/layering.test.ts` holds it — a view may not `fetch` (seven panels still do, listed by name in a list that may only shrink and cannot go stale), a store may not import a renderer, a host may not import React, a view may import a host's types but not its code, and a client may not reach more than its own route family |

The spawn area offloads only about 30 ms of main-thread time per 45 s, so the gain under terrain streaming is not measured yet |

Gold income: creatures drop gold from their `drops.gold` range straight to the killer's wallet; quests pay `gold` rewards on hand-in. Gateway: 0x21 trade + wallet + vendor + auction (protocol v23) — S→C trade state to the pair only, wallet balances to the owner only, vendor results to the actor, auction events to the parties; C→S trade/vendor/auction actions (Zod, pickup rate-limit bucket); admin `gold {amount}` grants. 52 tests green (37 unit + 15 gateway integration). Region side: `WorldClient.reportDungeonResults` sends the `instances.db` unreported batch over webrpc (`ReportDungeonResults`, validated), and the save timer flushes it with no overlapping requests, marking only what the world confirms. World side: `WorldCore.reportDungeonResults` records one `dungeon_run` row per run, idempotent on `instance`. Skill gains: commit trains the killer's weapon skill per kill. Chests: placed per run from the rules, opened in reach for chest-pool loot, HUD button. Traps: armed per run, sprung once under a member for the rules harm, named in the run state. Veins deferred to crafting actions (no material gathering exists yet) |
| D4b | Dungeon shell on the client: the baked shell (`<id>.shell.pcgm`) reaches the player as an ordinary asset, not a dungeon message | ✅ | The client drew only a stand-in floor ("walls aren't on the wire yet"). Same path as every PCG prop: the asset library takes prebaked-mesh assets (`addPrebaked`, no LOD chain), the instance spawns one static entity with `AssetRef(dungeon:<id>)` (replicated by the generic snapshot) and mounts the Pcg service (`mountPcgService`, extracted from `startPcgService`) on its own gateway; the pool index names each shell. Client: the Pcg client follows the gateway the client joined (`MirrorClient.endpoint`), so a hop to an instance fetches from the instance; the stand-in floor sits 6 cm lower. Dungeons baked without a shell (`--no-shell`, older bakes: 10 of 30 in a typical pool) run as before. Checked in the running client: walls, ceiling and floor draw and hold the player. D4 |
| D4d | Dungeon props: the baked pillars, furniture and torches stand in the run | ✅ | `content/dungeons/props.json` is the world's spawn table keyed `dungeon:<kind>` (39 kinds: asset, collision box by the world's convention, `lift` for mounted kinds). The pool reads layout props, dressing furniture (with the bake's footprint) and torches; the instance spawns each with `buildSpawn` + `spawnScatterEntity`, so the generic snapshot replicates them and the client batches them (438 in one draw call in the check). A kind with no entry is counted in `unspawnedProps`, not spawned; a test fails if the bake places a kind the table doesn't name. A modular part's position is its model's pivot (a wall's is at its top and one end), not its middle, and the support graph and the structure checks read it as the middle: 42% of the village preview's parts looked unsupported and the checks pruned 311 "detached" parts. Scene Structure nodes now carry `centreX/Y/Z` (the middle from the pivot, from the model bounds, in the part's axes); `placementBox`, the scene check and the collapse registration use it (checked against the real collider boxes: 4,197 parts, worst 1.4 mm). Preview after: 28 parts pruned (was 311), 107 of 8,201 parts (1.3%: props, a few roof tiles) do not stand at rest with the footing as ground (was 42%). The support graph also lets modules interpenetrate and treats a part whose contacts do not explain why it stands as bonded to what it touches. `closed` counts (12 of 49) were tuned on the wrong boxes and need a re-tune |
| DE4 | Collapse: unsupported parts fall (server-side step with a part budget per tick), take damage on landing, and the building's roof, upper floors and colliders follow; navigation and the places store update | 🚧 | Server side and live client play check done: `CollapseSystem` (`interact/collapse.ts`). A synthesized part is a body-less visual entity (the model, at its pivot) plus separate collider entities (`-col`, `-floor`, `-step-*`, `-jamb-*`, `-lintel`); when a part breaks (`BreakSystem` `onBreak`) or lacks support at registration (`!graph.stands[i]`), its colliders go immediately and the support graph says what lost its support. Those parts fall in waves (`waveDelayS`) within `perTick` (32) and `maxActive` (256) caps: each gets a dynamic carrier body at the model's middle (hidden like a collider) with outward topple impulse on multi-part buildings and a `groundY` floor clamp, the visual follows it offset by the pivot, and at rest (or after 12 s) a static carrier replaces it where it lies. Checked against real Jolt (`collapse-jolt.test.ts`), playtested on the village preview (`DESTRUCTION-PLAYTEST.md`, `scripts/collapse-playtest.mjs`: 21 breaks in one tick cost 0.6-1.8 ms, down from 82.7 ms), and verified live in headless Chromium on a 175-part synthesized `struct_12_Preset_House_06` (`144/144` upper parts collapsed `> 0.5 m` onto terrain colliders, `maxFinalDrop = 11.78 m`, `minY = -0.49 m`, `0` below `-2 m`). Open: `valgrind:perf` baseline on Linux/WSL. |

A record from another layout of the building (part count differs) is ignored and cleared. On the village preview: a 379-part demolition (358 fell, 21 broken) restarted with 369 of 379 parts at the same pose (the rest differ in the 2nd decimal), the 21 broken ones still gone, the same entity count (16,391) and nothing falling again. Found on the way and fixed: a part the break system removes while the scene spawns frees its slot for the next entity, so entity ids read after the spawn named somebody else; the scene now takes wire ids and poses at spawn time. 4 persistence tests + a slot-reuse regression test |

Targets with no body (creatures, props) are measured at their `Transform` as before. 4 combat tests, 1 collapse test; verified live in headless Chromium via client `Mouse0` melee strike against grounded modular wall `125` (pivot `py = 1.32 m`, `HP 200 → 199.15`). |

| DB1 | Dungeon shell corner modes: `cornerMode` on the shell and its theme (`square` default, `chamfered` cuts each closing corner at 45 degrees, `pillared` stands a square column on every corner of the floor plan) with `cornerSize` in metres. A corner is rock left standing, so its faces are walls and shell collision includes it. No built-in theme uses the modes yet and the editor panel has no control for them | ✅ | #696 |
| DB2 | Dungeon portals carry a width and a target level: `width` is how many wall cells the opening takes along the wall (every one is cut open and checked; two portals may not share a cell), `targetLevel` is the floor level it leads to. The editor's bake document, view and panel carry both, and the panel picks the portal's side from where the floor is | ✅ | #697 |
| DB3 | The dungeon bake panel is a view in the editor: Dungeons & PCG, Bake. Paint the layout, place a portal, pick the theme, edit a layer and re-bake; the view lists triangles, floor area, parts, portals, layer placements and provenance. The panel scrolls and its grid is the size of its cells | ✅ | #698 |
| DB4 | Dungeon walls have a thickness: `wallThickness` on the shell and its theme (0 default: the inside surface only, as before). Above 0 the rock is the space grown outwards by that much on every side, under every floor and over every ceiling, and its outside faces are in the shell as a fifth part, `outer`. The shell is then a closed body that reaches past the painted cells by the thickness. An open ceiling gets no roof slab. The outside is solid for collision | ✅ | #723 |
| DB5 | The terrain mask's path channel has roads. After the settlements route theirs, a path-only second pass (`bakeWorldPathMask`) bakes road polylines into `mask_path`: 3 m wide by default, then the spec's feather, padded so roads are continuous across chunk seams, with chunks no road reaches skipped. The world bake takes a `roads` callback; `bake-world` passes each fortified settlement's approach road. Base, rock and wet are byte-identical with or without roads | ✅ | this PR |

Jolt's own `Ragdoll` (11 bodies, 10 joints) at the engine's tick (two substeps, the addon's limits): 256 awake rigs take 25 ms (76% of the 33.3 ms tick); 1,000 take 110 ms with the contact cap filled, so that figure is not valid physics. The budget is 4 ms per tick, chosen 2026-10-31, and holds 48 full or 116 light awake rigs ([Performance and constraints](PERFORMANCE.md), [`ragdoll-cost-results.json`](ragdoll-cost-results.json), `npm run bench:ragdoll`) | ✅ | #488: One 4-vCPU container, up to about 35% between sweeps; re-measure on server hardware before the slice starts |

| — | **Dungeon physics**: instance bodies are swept against the grid walls (no corner clips, no tunnelling thin walls at speed), dressing lines carry `collision` (solid/none), the bake writes `colliders` (pillars, statues, chests; not torches or banners) that `DungeonWallSystem` enforces, spawns and the entrance are clear of walls and colliders, and the walkable grid agrees with the shell cell by cell | ✅ | `feat/physics-dungeons`; files baked before this have no `colliders` and need a re-bake |
| — | **Prop blocking without CharacterVirtual**: kinematic walkers resolve against static prop bodies on the JS side (`PropCollisionSystem`, after integrate/character, before the physics sync), so players no longer walk through scatter-spawned trees and rocks; scene nodes carry validated per-node `Collider` data (box/sphere/none, edited in the Scenes workspace) | ✅ | Merged via `integration/editor`; full CharacterVirtual stays 📋; `npm run valgrind:perf` still owes the new system a Linux baseline check |
| — | **Zero-Copy Hybrid Engine Data Plane**: page-aligned `SharedArrayBuffer` arena (`sab-store.ts`), 64B cache-line padded lockless SPSC command and transaction rings (`lockless-ring.ts`), 30Hz Simulation Worker (`sim-worker.ts`) with C++ N-API strided & SSE2 branchless Planar SoA SIMD kernels (`packages/addons/integrate` / `engine_kernel.node`), binary 4-byte frame `/ws/sim` ingestion gateway (`data-plane-server.ts`), and async Embedded SurrealDB Persistence Worker (`persist-worker.ts`, `schema.surrealql`) | ✅ | `HYBRID-DATA-PLANE.md`; PR #430 (`feat/zero-copy-hybrid-data-plane`); 22 unit + e2e + native addon tests green; persistence durable by default (#557, #562) |

Memcheck clean on all six addons; physics, procgen, scatter, geometry and navigation suites green locally |

The previous newest frame's `prior` is now cleared. | ✅ (#650; `net/interest.ts`, `test/net/interest-retention.test.ts`) | 200 players: retained heap 340 MB to 1.4 GB in 40 s before, 79 MB flat after; tick max 356 and 383 ms before, 145 and 72 ms after. Follow-up: rerun the 1,000-client GC numbers on main. |

Sustained 60 s, 3,000 NPCs: mean tick 30.6 to 16.2 ms at 200 players, 156.7 to 66.4 ms at 1,000; ticks completed at 500 players 42% to 86%. 1,000 players is still at 43% of ticks. | ✅ (#652; `docs/NATIVE-FANOUT.md`; the cited `perf/native-fanout-results-2026-11-03.md` is not in the repository) | Follow-ups: per-sector name versions to skip `selectNames` work, native `interestEntries` (15.5% of the 1,000-player profile), 1,000 players at 43% of ticks. |

Ids scanned per call fall from about 200,000 to about 4,000 at 1,000 players. | ✅ (#654; `docs/NATIVE-FANOUT.md`) | `selectNames` 5.9 to 0.77 ms per tick at 1,000 players; whole-tick mean 74 to 65 ms in single runs. `assemble` (about 8 ms) is now the larger native piece. |

New workload `prop-collision-wide` (5,000 props on 200 m): 8.8 M per tick. The Node 22 baseline in `perf/callgrind-baseline.json` was not updated (see the log). |

The terrain mask goldens, which passed on Intel and failed on the AMD CI runners, are re-pinned and pass on both. Worlds baked before this differ from worlds baked after it | ✅ | #695 |

BitECS `net/interest.ts` retains its `SectorFrame` + native `addon-fanout` pipeline (`PF1`–`PF3`). |

## 5b. Edge / core split

Doc: [`EDGE-CORE-SPLIT.md`](internals/EDGE-CORE-SPLIT.md). Its E1–E6 are the
split's slices, not the editor's E items.

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E1 | Edge skeleton + generic packet router (`apps/edge`, `content/edge/routes.json`) | ✅ | (#83) |
| E2 | Internal channel edge↔core (`packages/channel`): framed TCP, heartbeats, reconnect | ✅ | (#89) |
| E3 | Core serves the edge channel; durable sessions; tick, delta and snapshot pushes | ✅ | Additive: players still connect to the core (#100) |
| E3b | Client cutover: the edge serves the client protocol, the core drops its player listener | ✅ | `feat/edge-cutover`, reviving `feat/edge-core-split` (which only removed the listener). With `MMO_EDGE=1` the core's gateway runs on `ChannelTransport` (a third Transport beside uws and ws): the edge's game port (`EDGE_GAME_PORT`, `game-relay.ts`) holds the players' WebSocket and HTTP and relays them over the channel (`wire.*`, `http`), so the core binds no player port. `npm start` runs it so, with the edge on 7777; a core started alone still listens directly (tests, editor play). Checked live: hello, welcome, snapshot, deltas and `/health` through the edge. Sockets close on a core restart (1012) and are not held through it yet |
| E4 | Degraded mode and resync on the edge | ✅ | The edge holds players through a core outage (#101) |
| E5 | Measure the split (`npm run bench:e5`, [`EDGE-SPLIT-RESULTS.md`](internals/EDGE-SPLIT-RESULTS.md)) | ✅ | 0 hung requests through crash, restart and freeze; mirror encoding a little over budget (#108) |
| E5a | Binary mirror frames on the channel | ✅ | |
| E5b | Push encode off the tick (if E5a is not enough) | not needed | |
| E5c | Drop the unused base64 mirror stream (E3b follow-up) | ✅ | `perf/drop-tick-delta-push`. Since E3b players get their snapshots and deltas as relayed `wire.*` frames, so the core stops pushing `tick.delta` every tick and a full `snapshot` every 300th; `tick.events`, requests and heartbeats stay. The edge drops its unread mirror and tick buffers and the two `mmo_edge_mirror_*` metrics (the Grafana panel now shows relayed frames). B3 added tick cost 0.69 → 0.23 ms (median of 7). Makes E5a/E5b moot |
| IM | **Interest management**: per-sector deltas encoded once and shared, per-client interest sets with hysteresis, enter/leave via one `DeltaSet` per client per tick assembled at the edge, pinned entities (own avatar, party, targets, quest NPCs), interest snapshots, `all` mode for the debugger and admins, cross-region view at borders | ✅ | Spec: `2026-10-23-interest-management-design.md`. IM0 B7 bench (`feat/im0-bench`); IM1 one publish per tick (`feat/im1-one-publish`); IM2 `DeltaSet` (`feat/im2-deltaset`); IM3 per-sector encode and interest sets behind `MMO_INTEREST=1` (`feat/im3-interest`); IM4 edge assembly (`feat/im4-edge-assembly`); IM5 pins and events (`feat/im5-pins-events`); IM6 `all` mode, debugger toggle, on by default (`feat/im6-all-mode`); IM8 cross-region view (`feat/im8-border-view`). IM7 (rate LOD) not needed by B7 |

## 6. World server

Doc: [World server](WORLD-SERVER.md) §12

| # | Item | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Core and registry: `WorldCore`, SurrealDB schema, webrpc, region auth, heartbeats | ✅ |
| B | Region migration (border hand-off) | ✅ (arbitration #119, live hand-off #122) |
| C | Accounts on the world server | ✅ | #618: the world server owns accounts (SurrealDB `account` table, argon2id hashes, webrpc GetAccount and CreateAccount); the region signs players in through a world-backed credential store and copies CSV-only accounts up. |
| D | Stress harness and the published number | ✅ (`npm run stress:world`, [Performance and constraints](PERFORMANCE.md), #120) |
| PL1 | World ingestion accepts real region state: request bodies are sized from the schema caps (snapshots 4 MiB, ledger batches 1 MiB, character state 256 KiB; everything else 256 KiB) inside the webrpc router. Before, the global 256 KB parser was below them, and a 1,377-entity region's snapshot (257 KB in base64) was ~23 entities from being refused on every upload. And snapshots come due: the shipper keeps a range's upload slot (every 300 ticks plus its phase) when a fresher save takes over. Before, the region offered every 1 s save, each offer pushed the slot back, and no snapshot ever reached the world (checked live: 40 s of a real region, 0 rows) | ✅ (`fix/world-body-limit`) |
| PL2 | The world server embeds SurrealDB (`@surrealdb/node`, RocksDB in its data dir; no separate DB server), and the SurrealDB store contracts run in `npm test` | ✅ (`feat/world-embedded-surreal`): default `WORLD_DB_URL` is `rocksdb:<WORLD_DATA_DIR>/surreal`; `mem://` embeds in memory; `ws://` still reaches a server; `mem:` is the plain JS store. Four store contracts run embedded by default; the define-functions check still needs a server (it opens several clients on one database) |
| PL3 | Regions are hot caches: the periodic snapshot upload carries all region state (quests, gathering pouches and node stocks, guilds, social, parties), and a region rebuilt from the world store gets it all back. Spec: place state rides the range snapshot, per-character state the character record, guilds and parties become world records | ✅ PL3a (`feat/region-places`: node stocks ride the snapshot as `places` and recall seeds them back); PL3b (`feat/character-extras`: quest log and pouch ride the character's world record as `extras` and seed at login); PL3c (`feat/social-world-records`: guilds, parties, friends and blocks are `social_record`s on the world, written through on the save timer, version-guarded, refreshed every 60 s; world ids from 1,000,000,000, local ones renumbered at the first fleet boot) |
| PL4 | `npm start -- --world` (opt in; plain `npm start` stays standalone) runs the world server: dev runs persist to its embedded SurrealDB (`<data>/world-server`). The region registers with an explicit range covering the whole baked world (`world_bounds`; an assigned range starts at chunk 0,0 and would miss three quarters of an origin-centred world); `--standalone` keeps the old local-only run | ✅ (`feat/dev-world-server`) |
| PL5 | SurrealMX adoption (2026-10-28): SurrealDB engines aligned to 3.x (`@surrealdb/node` ^3.1.0, SDK ^2.2.0); every `mem://` path runs SurrealMX; the world tier's recommended durable shape is the SurrealMX server (`npm run db`, AOL + snapshots, AOL recovery drilled); the embedded default moves RocksDB → SurrealKV and new world catalogs are written on SurrealKV. Measured constraint: embedded `mem://` ignores MX persistence parameters, so MX is never the embedded default | ✅ local (`arlo/surreal-mx`, not yet pushed): `SURREALMX-ADOPTION-REPORT.md` |
| PL6 | Batched character-state writes (2026-10-28): single-statement upserts replace check-then-write; store batch reads/writes; `WorldCore.putCharacterStates` with per-record outcomes; the save path coalesces bursts into one bulk world call (10 ms window, logout immediate, per-record fallback when the world predates the bulk op). Measured ~5.5× the old write path at 500 records (embedded MX, sandbox) | ✅ local (`arlo/surreal-mx`, not yet pushed): [`../packages/world-server/PERFORMANCE-BATCH.md`](../packages/world-server/PERFORMANCE-BATCH.md) |
| PL7 | Region database renamed `world.db` to `region.db`: the region process's embedded SQLite file is a hot cache, with the world server as the source of truth when `WORLD_URL` is set. An existing `world.db` (with its WAL, SHM and play backup) is renamed on first open, and an existing `region.db` is never overwritten. Terrain, chunk entities, formation tiles, sessions and the item ledger stay in `region.db`; moving them to the world server needs a decision. | ✅ (`persist/region-db.ts`, `scripts/lib/region-db.mjs`) |
| PL8 | Data-plane ledger defaults to the SurrealMX server (`npm run db`, `ws://127.0.0.1:8000/rpc`, AOL with fsync per commit): the zero-copy persist worker no longer writes embedded RocksDB by default. Escape hatches: `MMO_DATA_PLANE_STORE=rocksdb` restores RocksDB, `mem://` stays the volatile bench opt-out. Boot rejects when the server is unreachable. Benchmark: p99 tick under 0.5 ms against a 33.3 ms budget. | ✅ (#645) |

| WX2 | Incremental, content-addressed world bake | ✅ | The bake materializes the whole world (4 km = 103 MB, 16 km ~1.5 GB, 32 km ~6 GB, linear in area) and 80% of it is `terrain_tiles` — one float32 heightmap per 16 m chunk. [World building](WORLD-BUILDING.md): the tiles are a derived content-addressed cache, not part of the bake — `makeTileChunkSource` already falls back to procgen when a tile is absent, so the server runs with no tiles at all. The survey is content-addressed by a hash of the spec (skipped when unchanged); tiles are keyed by `(seed, cx, cz, lod, terrainVersion, surveyHash)` in a `terrain_tile_keys` table reusing the existing `heightsFingerprint()`; and `bake:world` gains `--region rx,rz` and `--survey-only` with the per-chunk tile loop parallelized across worker threads. `regionsPerSide` already partitions the world square. The survey stays global and iterative (erosion), so a spec change re-runs it — the win is that tiles are no longer rewritten for free, only the affected regions. |
| WX1 | Weather as a field: conditions emerge, not scripts | ✅ | Coarse moisture/temperature/wind field over the world map, advanced in a client web worker (existing `WorkerPool`), deterministic from (seed, step) so every client agrees with no messages and no replication. Grid is its own 250–500 m lattice (4k–16k cells at a 16–32 km world), not the terrain grid — simulate coarse, render fine. The baked `terrain/climate.ts` solve stays the static climatology, with a relaxation term so the field cannot drift. Client-side by design: the server has no business knowing how a surface is lit, and the existing weather already derives everything from (biome, tick). Stages: field sim core + worker handler → map/climate plumbing → sky overcast + cloud volumes + rain shader → couplings (wet look, ripples, wind, audio). Looks stay in `content/materials/materials.json`; who is shaded stays a shading component. Climate is sourced: `FieldService` takes a `ClimateParams` block and `world-canvas.tsx` reads it from the welcome's `WorldInfo` (additive — falls back to `DEFAULT_CLIMATE` until the server sends it). Remaining couplings: wet look, ripples, weather audio. |
| WG1 | Land share: `land.coast.waterEdge` 0.4 in the default world gives about 74% land on the 4 km world (was 52% at 0.6) | ✅ | Measured with the survey alone (`bake-world --survey-only`); 0.3 is below the spec's limit; the world is rebaked with `npm run bake:world:force` (stop the server first) |
| WG2 | Humidity dial: `climate.humidity` (0..2, default 1) scales the rain and the moisture of air over the land | ✅ | Rain scales about with the square of the dial: 0.5 gives a quarter of the default; `test/procgen/climate-humidity.test.ts` |

One piece per entity, solid. Art pattern `SM_Prop_Fence_01-04`, fitted to its piece length like a wall (x1.01, so pieces meet end to end). The scene baker no longer authors a prop thinner than the scene format's 0.1 m (a fence at building scale 2 made the scene invalid). Rules `settlement-rules/12`, generator `0.9.0`. Town 0001-sized (668 people): 93 lines, 558 pieces, every gate passes. Entity cost is the price: about 6 per line; turn it off with `fences.enabled` | ✅ | SG9; tests `test/fences.test.ts`; rebake to see it |
| SG13 | Every model spawns, every stand-in filled: measured first. Of 65 structure, wall and prop kinds only 9 had no model (theatre, cathedral, monastery, barracks, library, mine, master fishery, fish rack, fishing spot); fallbacks now cover all but `mine`, `fish_rack` and `fishing_spot` (no model in the packs; named in a test so a new gap cannot hide). The places scene was boxes by construction: `places-cli` had no catalog option and the bake never gave it one (53 of 53 boxes); it takes `--catalog` now, with place patterns (guard outpost, roadside inn, shack, farmhouse) and the farm packs' dressing for fields, camp sites and yards (0 of 52 boxes after a rebake). A shack takes a model no town kind uses, because a model has one scale for the whole world. `test/art-coverage.test.ts` checks every kind against the local catalog (skips without the licensed art) | ✅ | SG9, SG12; `MMO_CATALOG` or `assets/catalog.json` |

## 7b. Engine core / game IP split

Doc: [`ENGINE-CORE-SPLIT.md`](ENGINE-CORE-SPLIT.md): where the line
between the engine and our game runs today, the three-tier alpha skeleton
(core / kits / game) and the nine staged migration steps. Stages `ES1a`
to `ES4` are additive and break nothing; `ES5` to `ES9` move code. Each
row is one PR.

| # | Item | Status | Needs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ES0 | Design: inventory every package and module classified core / SDK / kit / IP, the alpha skeleton, and the staged plan with risks. Every code claim cited by file and line against `d9fe367` | ✅ | Analysis only, no code moved. **Decided 2026-11-22**: engine core plus official kits, and the kits are *several* forkable starter packs rather than one blessed template — the brief is the fastest and most flexible route to someone else's game, including one that replaces our kits entirely |
| ES1a | `private: true` on the five packages that lack it (`mirror` — which declares `UNLICENSED` — `addons/geometry`, `addons/navigation`, `addons/perf-probe`, `addons/physics`), so none can publish by accident. Nothing about licensing | ✅ | Independent of the licence choice: unlicensed code publishing by accident is the bad outcome whichever licence is later picked. Measured on `main` first — each of the five produced a full tarball (physics 1,222 files with vendored Jolt, navigation 217 with vendored Recast/Detour, mirror 15 while declaring `UNLICENSED`); after the change each `npm publish --dry-run` ends in "Skipping workspace …, marked as private". Note `npm pack` still packs a private package — `npm publish` is the check that matters |
| ES1b | `exports` maps on the packages that go public, `license` fields, a root `LICENSE`, and an aggregated third-party notices file | ⏭️ | **Parked**: the person deferred the licence choice on 2026-11-22 pending the call on whether this is a monetized venture. Each item here states how others may consume the code, so each waits. The notices file is worth producing as *input* to that call, since the vendored libraries (Jolt, Recast, FastNoise2, manifold, Clipper2, Dungeon Forge) constrain it |
| ES2 | `game.json` and its resolver in a new `@mmo-engine/sdk`: 34 seams in one table (`packages/sdk/src/seams.ts`), each with its `MMO_*` override and its default, plus a manifest a game writes instead of moving paths one variable at a time. `server.ts` boots through it, so the 9 `REPO_ROOT` content paths and the `MMO_* || DEFAULT_*` pairs are gone from it | ✅ | With no manifest every path is the constant the engine already had: `packages/engine-core/test/game-project-defaults.test.ts` imports the 15 exported constants and asserts each one, and covers the rest against `server.ts` and `packages/settlement/src/paths.ts` as text. The `MMO_*` variables still win over the manifest. Boot checked both ways: engine defaults, and a manifest outside the checkout with two moved seams and its hooks module, both reaching `listening on ws://`. Follow-up `ES2b` |
| ES3 | Reachable rule hooks, delivered as `hooks.mjs` beside a game's rules (`MMO_RULES_HOOKS` names one elsewhere, `0` runs the engine's defaults), default-exporting the overrides or a factory handed the loaded rules. `openGameRules` finds it once and carries it into every later load, so a hot reload keeps a game's hooks instead of dropping back to engine defaults | ✅ | Done ahead of `ES2`, so the entry is `hooks.mjs` rather than `game.json`'s `hooks` key; `ES2` will route it through `game.json`. The eight hooks in `packages/rules/src/hooks.ts` existed unreachable: `server.ts` and the reloader both called `loadGameRules` with no overrides. 17 tests, two of which read `server.ts` and fail if either call site drops the argument again. `content/rules/hooks.example.mjs` is a working example; `MMO_RULES_HOOKS` is in [CONFIGURATION.md](CONFIGURATION.md). `onSpawn`/`onLoot` stay open |
| ES2b | The three resolvers become one: `packages/settlement/src/paths.ts` and `scripts/lib/bake-paths.mjs` delegate to `@mmo-engine/sdk` instead of keeping their own copies of the `MMO_*` table, and the `DEFAULT_*` constants in the libraries take the project's path rather than deriving their own | 📋 | ES2. The CLIs keep their flags, which still win; `npm run bake:all` byte-identical, and the 6-seed town/route/place counts unchanged |
| ES2c | `graphify --check` says which part of the artifact is stale. The check compares the whole file, so editing any of the 317 files in `meta.sourceHashes` fails it while the graph itself is unchanged — and the message printed `meta.graphHash` alone, the same string twice, which reads like a bug in the checker | ✅ | Split out of `ES2b`. The failure now names the cause: the graph's hash when it moved (with the counts that moved with it), the edited, new and gone source files when it did not, capped at 8 names plus a count, then other `meta` keys and other top-level keys. 6 new assertions over the pure explainer plus an end-to-end `--check` run on a stale artifact |
| ES4 | Typed service registry: the 18 `assembled.byName[...]` and 4 `assembled.extensions[...]` reads are gone from `server.ts`, replaced by one table (`packages/engine-core/src/services.ts`) naming the module behind each service, system or extension, and whether the server runs without it. Plus a `modules` block in `game.json` that layers under the host's own wiring for each module | ✅ | The 11 objects at `server.ts:1394` stay: they are host wiring (callbacks, live engine objects), not configuration, so what moves to the manifest is the data keys — a key the host sets wins (that is where a `MMO_*` variable lands), one it leaves unset comes from the file, one neither sets is the module's default. Booted three ways: defaults, a manifest setting `sample-heartbeat.everyTicks` to 45 (the ring log shows 45, 90, 135 instead of 600), and the `dungeons` module disabled, which now stops the boot with "enable dungeons in the module set" instead of pushing an `undefined` into the gateway's extension list. 46 tests; `dropperRef`, `questsRef` and `physicsRef` take their real types now that no cast stands in the way |
| ES4b | The engine boots with none of a game's modules: the service table's required list goes from 11 to 3. `items`, `dropper`, `wallet`, `body`, `magic`, `creatures`, `dungeons` and `quests` become optional, and the wiring that assumed them (the gateway's extension list, its items and creature adapters, the respawn pool's creature read) treats each as a feature that is simply absent | ✅ | ES4. Measured: `core` alone now reports exactly `physics`, `character` and `mastery` missing, where it reported 11; `core`, `physics`, `character` reaches `listening`, ticks for 70 s to tick 2,055 and saves on SIGTERM with nothing in the ring log. A test pins the required list to those three, so a stage that re-couples the engine to a game's design fails there rather than at `ES8`'s kit-less CI job. The lazy accessors now say which module to enable instead of "not yet assembled" |
| ES4c | The gateway reads its systems by port, not by class: `net/ports.ts` declares the six it asks of a game's combat (`combat`, `life`, `abilities`, `telegraphs`, `statuses`, `duels`) with the methods it calls on each, systems offer a port by name (`@mmo-engine/sdk`'s `PORTS`), and `openPorts` resolves them at boot. The 10 `instanceof` scans and the six value imports are gone from `net/gateway.ts` | ✅ | ES4. A system that offers a port it cannot answer ends the boot with every problem named; a port nothing offers is a feature this run lacks, not an error. The gateway's default system list no longer builds a `CombatSystem`. Guards: compile-time conformance per class, the table against the real prototypes, and a source check that `gateway.ts` cannot go back to `instanceof`. Follow-up `ES4d` |
| ES4d | The same for the economy and world systems the gateway found by class: `TradeSystem`, `WalletSystem`, `AuctionSystem`, `VendorSystem`, `GatheringSystem`, `ChestSystem`, `BreakSystem`, `ResidentSystem` — 20 sites, including the five `private get` accessors. 14 ports in the table now, and no `instanceof` of a system class anywhere in `net/gateway.ts` | ✅ | ES4c's mechanism, second set. These eight are declared as `Pick<>` of the system that answers them today rather than retyped by hand, because the gateway calls 70-odd methods across them and a hand-written signature that widened one would be a bug the compiler then stops finding. `PortDecl` gained `fields` for the data a host reads rather than calls (`gathering.size` is a getter, `castSeconds` an own field), which the boot check had no way to see before |
| ES5a | The shared vocabulary leaves the combat systems: `Weapon` and `LifeEvent` move to `packages/engine-core/src/vocabulary.ts`, which imports the rules package and nothing of the engine's. Nine files across items, quests, dungeons, creatures and the server imported `combat.ts` or `death.ts` only to name a type | ✅ | ES4b, and a prerequisite for `ES5`: a kit cannot take a system out of `engine-core` while the rest of the engine imports that system's module for its vocabulary. Pure type move, so nothing changes at runtime, and the package index still exports both from the same place. A test reads every source file and fails if either type is declared elsewhere, read from anywhere but `vocabulary.js`, or if `vocabulary.ts` grows an engine import. What is left crossing the boundary: the gateway and `dungeons/instance.ts` find six systems by `instanceof`, and `systems.ts`, `admin/*` and `stats/stamina-system.ts` read the `Injury`, `Body`, `Wound` and `Mana` components |
| ES5b | What a system announces joins the vocabulary: `HitEvent`, `AbilityEvent`/`AbilityState`/`TriggerResult`, `TelegraphEvent`/`TelegraphShape`, `StatusChange` and `DuelEvent`/`DuelEndReason` move out of the five systems into `vocabulary.ts`. The gateway's six combat ports now name no system at all, not even as a type import | ✅ | ES5a, ES4c. The systems import their own events like everyone else; the package index exports all eleven shared types from one place, so nothing outside the engine moves. Gate: a source test asserts each name is declared only in `vocabulary.ts`, read only from `vocabulary.js`, exported from the index, and that `net/ports.ts` imports none of `combat.js`, `death.js`, `abilities.js`, `telegraph.js`, `status.js`, `duel.js` |
| ES5c | The component seam: `Body`, `Injury` and `Mana` move from `body.ts`/`magic.ts` into `components.ts`, with `Wound`, `BLEEDING_BIT`, `NO_JUMP` and `PART_STATES`. The engine's own tick no longer imports a kit's module to read what that kit's systems wrote — `systems.ts` (movement, jumping), `stats/stamina-system.ts` (recovery), `admin/inspect.ts` and `admin/live-overlay.ts` | ✅ | ES5b. A component is a layout in a SharedArrayBuffer, not a type, so the move is gated on the layout as well as the imports: a new test pins all three field for field (name, order, typed array, per-entity length, still SAB-backed). Same-container callgrind A/B: `sim-tick-1000` +0.16%, `residents-tick` −0.3%, both inside noise. `BodyConfig`, `Health` and `Stamina` were already here |
| ES5 | Kits: `@mmo-engine/kit-combat`, `-progress`, `-economy`, `-quests`, `-dungeons`, `-social`. About 13,000 LOC moves out of `engine-core/src`; modules import the kit instead of `../../packages/engine-core/dist/src/*.js`. Also inverts the skill contract so a kit declares its own required ids rather than the engine demanding `parrying` of every game. Each kit is built to be forked and edited, not only configured: small files, no cross-kit imports, and a README naming the hook or system to change for the common edits | 📋 | ES4. One kit per PR, pure moves with no behaviour change in the same commit. Extra gate: the kit still builds with one of its systems deleted, which is what keeps the kits separable instead of one template in six folders |
| ES6 | Civic split: the mechanisms (bridge wear, escrow contracts, item provenance, foundry specialization, vein surveying) become `kit-logistics`; the Redwater Crossing names and rules move to the game pack. 5,566 LOC with the deepest coupling in the repo (`net/gateway.ts`, `net/health.ts`, `items/item-service.ts`, `pools/*`) | 📋 | ES5. `civic.test.ts`, `civic-e2e.test.ts`, `civic-world-tracks.test.ts` and `fleet-telemetry.test.ts` green, plus a live bridge restoration |
| ES7 | Our game into `games/nexus/`: `content/` (including 4.5 MB of baked settlements), our HUD panels out of `apps/client/src/ui`, and the dev tools (`dev-panels.tsx`, `perf-hud.tsx`, `debug.ts`, `admin-console.tsx`) out of the player client into the editor's play mode, per `CLAUDE.md` | 📋 | ES6. `npm run bake:all` byte-identical; the 6-seed town/route/place counts unchanged |
| ES8 | `create-mmo-game` as a template picker (`bare` core-only, `survival`, `sandbox`, `full`), plus de-IP-ing the engine schemas: `procgen/wildlife-table.ts:110`'s `yardsticks: z.strictObject({ goblin, orc, troll, golem })` becomes an open map, and `server.ts:1352`'s hardcoded `["bandit","raider","goblin","wolf"]` ambushers come from rules. `apps/client` splits into `client-runtime` and the game client | 📋 | ES7. Two CI jobs: a walkable world with one creature in under five minutes and zero engine files edited, **and** `bare` booting with no kit installed at all — that second one is the regression test for the whole split. Existing `content/wildlife` still validates |
| ES9 | Editor as an engine tool: the panel registry loads from the open project at runtime instead of the build-time Vite glob at `apps/editor/src/ui/modules-panel.tsx:32`, and the hardcoded `WORKSPACES` array (`App.tsx:88`) becomes a registry a module can add a workspace to | 📋 | ES2 for `game.json`; folds into the planned editor rebuild rather than being done twice |

## Update log

Newest first: add your line directly below this one, not at the end of the file.
One line per merged PR or decision.
- **2026-11-22**: Post-merge fix. `#729` renamed quadruped bone `0` from `body` to `chest` in `BONE_NAMES` (`packages/animal-rig/src/segment.ts:36`) and left `apps/editor/test/rig-preview.test.ts:47` asserting the old name, so `build-test` was red on `main` and on every branch cut after it. One assertion, now reading `chest`; the editor suite is 996 of 996 with it. The panel itself was already correct -- it renders whatever `BONE_NAMES` holds -- so nothing shipped was wrong, only the test that guards it.

- **2026-11-22**: `ES4c` done. The gateway no longer finds a system by its class. `net/ports.ts` declares the six ports it reads of a game's combat — `combat`, `life`, `abilities`, `telegraphs`, `statuses`, `duels` — each with the methods the gateway calls on it; a system offers a port by name through `@mmo-engine/sdk`'s `PORTS` symbol, and `openPorts` resolves the lot once at boot. That removes the 10 `instanceof` scans and, with them, the six value imports of `CombatSystem`, `DeathSystem`, `AbilitySystem`, `StatusSystem`, `TelegraphSystem` and `DuelSystem` from `net/gateway.ts` — the imports that stood between those systems and a kit package of their own, since a class you look for by identity has to be in your own build. The gateway's default system list stopped building a `CombatSystem` too (`StatsSystem`, `IntentSystem`, `IntegrateSystem` remain; every test that needs combat already passes its own systems). Fail-closed, like the `ES4` registry: a system that offers a port without the methods for it ends the boot naming the system and the methods, while a port nothing offers is simply a feature this run does not have. Guards: compile-time conformance of each class to its port, the table checked against the real systems, and a source check so `gateway.ts` cannot quietly go back to `instanceof`. 271 net tests pass unchanged, which is the behaviour proof: the hits, duels and ability paths run through the ports now. Follow-up `ES4d` for the eight economy and world systems the gateway still scans for.

- **2026-11-22**: `ES5a` done, clearing the way for the kits. `Weapon` and `LifeEvent` are the two types the engine and a game's systems have to agree on with no system behind them — what a striker swings, and what loot, quests and a dungeon run all react to — and they were declared inside `combat.ts` and `death.ts`. Nine files imported a combat system's module only to name one, which is why a kit could not have taken those systems out of `engine-core` without the engine following. They now live in `packages/engine-core/src/vocabulary.ts`, which imports `@mmo-engine/rules` and nothing of the engine's, so a kit reads the vocabulary from the engine and the engine never reads the kit. A pure type move: nothing changes at runtime and the package index still exports both. Four tests read every source file and fail if either type is declared elsewhere, imported from anywhere but `vocabulary.js`, or if `vocabulary.ts` grows an engine import. Still crossing the boundary, and what `ES5` proper now faces: `net/gateway.ts` and `dungeons/instance.ts` find `CombatSystem`, `DeathSystem`, `AbilitySystem`, `StatusSystem`, `TelegraphSystem` and `DuelSystem` by `instanceof`, and `systems.ts`, `admin/player.ts`, `admin/inspect.ts`, `admin/live-overlay.ts` and `stats/stamina-system.ts` read the `Injury`, `Body`, `Wound` and `Mana` components.

- **2026-11-22**: `ES2c` done, split out of `ES2b`. `graphify --check` compares the artifact's whole bytes, so editing any of the 317 files in `meta.sourceHashes` fails it while the graph is unchanged — and the message printed `meta.graphHash` alone, which showed the same hash twice and read like a bug in the checker rather than a stale artifact. It now names the cause: the hashes and the counts that moved when the graph itself changed, or the edited, new and gone source files when it did not (8 names, then a count), then any other `meta` key and any other top-level key that differs, and the formatting case where nothing differs by value. Touching one tracked file now prints `edited (1): apps/editor/src/authoring/relevance.ts`. The explainer is a pure function, exported and covered by 6 assertions, plus an end-to-end `--check` run against a stale copy of the real artifact.

- **2026-11-22**: `ES4b` done, and it is the first measured result of the split. The engine no longer needs a game's modules to run: the service table's required list is `physics`, `character` and `mastery` — three, where it was eleven. A set of `core`, `physics`, `character` reaches `listening on ws://`, ticks for 70 s to tick 2,055 and saves on SIGTERM with nothing in the ring log, with 15 of the 19 services off (`items`, `dropper`, `wallet`, `vendor`, `body`, `magic`, `telegraphs`, `combat`, `creatures`, `bots`, `crowd`, `residents`, `dungeons`, `quests`, `civic`). `core` alone names exactly the three it still wants. What had to change was small and all in `server.ts`: the gateway's extension list filters the absent ones instead of carrying nulls, its items and creature adapters answer empty rather than reaching for a system that is not there, and the respawn pool reads creatures through the same accessor as everything else. A test pins the required list to those three, so a later stage that re-couples the engine to a game's design fails there rather than in `ES8`'s kit-less CI job.

- **2026-11-22**: `ES4` done. `server.ts` no longer reaches into the assembled modules by string: one table in `packages/engine-core/src/services.ts` names each of the 19 services its entry point reads, the module behind it, whether it is a tick system or a gateway extension, and whether the server runs without it; `@mmo-engine/sdk` holds the mechanism so a game with its own entry point writes its own table. All 22 casts are gone, and with them the class of bug they hid — a service a module stopped providing was an `undefined` that surfaced as a `TypeError` in the first tick that touched it. Disabling the `dungeons` module now ends the boot with `enable dungeons in the module set`; the boot line reads `[modules] services: 17/19, off: bots, residents`. `game.json` also gains a `modules` block, layered under whatever the host passes a module, so a `MMO_*` variable still wins for one run and a game's own module is configured from the file with no engine change (`modules/sample-heartbeat` reads `everyTicks` as the worked example). The 11 config objects at `server.ts:1394` stayed: they are closures and live engine objects, not data a JSON file can hold.
- **2026-11-22**: `WL2` anatomy-rig branch (`#729`) merged, with two defects fixed at merge time rather than asserted away. (1) The wide pack scatter it added applied to every `spawnPack` caller, which moved hand-placed camps off their scene spot: the spread is now opt-in through a `PackSpread` argument (`creatures.ts`), ambient wildlife asks for `{ mode: "grazing" }` (`procgen/wild-creatures.ts`) and everything else keeps the tight 1.5-3 m clump, covered by a new anchored case in `creature-wander.test.ts` beside the grazing one. (2) `attachDefaultCollider` read `Transform.sx` to size a character capsule, and because entity ids are recycled a character spawned into a scaled prop's old slot inherited that prop's scale (a walker got 3x its radius, stopping 0.7 m early in `scale.test.ts`); the caller now passes the scale and `systems.ts` clamps it to [1/16, 16]. Also dropped the branch's ad-hoc path probing for `DEFAULT_WILDLIFE_TABLE`/`DEFAULT_NATURAL_WEAPONS` (and `scale.ts`/`scene-spawn.ts`), which ES2's resolver now owns and which the ES2 gate test caught disagreeing with it. Engine-core 1,971 tests, 0 fail, 2 skipped; `graphify --check` byte-identical; `npm run lint` clean.
- **2026-11-22**: `ES2` done. A game says where its content is in a `game.json`, and the engine reads every path through `@mmo-engine/sdk`'s resolver: 34 seams in one table, each with the `MMO_*` variable that overrides it for a run and the default it has always had. `server.ts` no longer builds content paths from `REPO_ROOT`, and the manifest can name the `ES3` hooks module. Order is variable, then manifest, then the engine's default; with no manifest nothing moves, which 22 tests assert by importing the engine's own constants. Booted both ways, including a manifest outside the checkout. Found while wiring it: the biome registry, the ecology plan and the wildlife tables ship inside `packages/engine-core/content/`, not under `content/`, so they are engine-bundled sample data a game points away from. Follow-up `ES2b` for the two resolvers that still keep their own copy of the table.

- **2026-11-22**: DOC3 follow-up — the client physics backend is documented as PhysX alone (`docs/CLIENT.md`): PhysX is the default, the choice is justified by the measured 200-rig comparison, and `rapier-world.ts` is named once as the second implementation of the same seam rather than a co-equal option. `docs/BUILDING-A-GAME.md` reworded so the cheapest-layer guidance reads as a cost ordering, not a restriction: the engine ships as source and every layer is the user's to change, with the upgrade cost of each stated instead. The "not licensed for release" bullet removed from `docs/CAPABILITIES.md` (licensing is an open decision; the docs should not state terms). The rules hooks are now described as working, with a dated note that the `server.ts` overrides wiring lands separately.
- **2026-11-22**: DOC3 follow-up 2 — `docs/CLIENT.md` documents both client physics backends as a supported choice, per the person: PhysX is the default (`createClientPhysicsWorld()`) and Rapier is selected with `createClientPhysicsWorld("rapier")`. Neither is a fallback for the other, and the basis for choosing is in the doc as a table — 200 awake rigs: PhysX 15.7 ms / ~2.0 MB gz / does not run under Node, Rapier 32.9 ms / ~1.6 MB gz / runs under Node, because the pinned `physx-js-webidl` build is web-compiled. That last row is why the three client physics suites run on Rapier, which the doc now tells anyone wanting physics in a test. `docs/BUILDING-A-GAME.md` §3 matches the hooks shape landing in #743.
- **2026-11-22**: Client physics is a documented choice between two supported backends, not a default with a fallback (the person's call: "We can make both options available to users to choose from as a backend Physics option"). `createClientPhysicsWorld(backend)` keeps taking either, PhysX stays the default, and both `index.ts` and the roadmap row now carry what each costs so a dev chooses on evidence: 200 awake rigs, PhysX 15.7 ms per tick and ~2.0 MB gzipped against Rapier 32.9 ms and ~1.6 MB. Rapier is also the only one that runs outside a browser — the pinned `physx-js-webidl` build is web-compiled and answers "not compiled for this environment" under Node — which is why the three physics suites exercise the seam through it. Fixed the packaging bug the dependency audit found as G3: `physx-js-webidl` was a `devDependency` of `apps/client` while shipped code imported it and its `.wasm` at `src/physics/physx-world.ts:41,47`, and is now a dependency. Client suite 568 of 569 pass, 1 skipped, 0 fail.
- **2026-11-22**: `ES3` done. The eight rule hooks are reachable from a booted server for the first time: `openGameRules` finds a game's `hooks.mjs` beside its rules (or at `MMO_RULES_HOOKS`, or none at `0`), accepts either an overrides object or a factory handed the loaded rules, checks every key against the SDK's own `HOOK_NAMES` so a misspelled hook stops the boot instead of silently doing nothing, and returns a loader that carries the overrides into every later load. `server.ts` boots through it and hands it to `RulesReloader`, so a hot reload no longer drops a game back to the engine's defaults and a factory re-runs against the reloaded rules. 17 tests in `packages/engine-core/test/rules-hook-overrides.test.ts`, two of which read `server.ts` and fail if either call site loses the argument again. Added `content/rules/hooks.example.mjs`, a hooks section in `content/rules/README.md`, and the `MMO_RULES_HOOKS` row in [CONFIGURATION.md](CONFIGURATION.md). Hooks are the cheap path and not a fence: a game is free to fork or replace any of it.
- **2026-11-22**: `DEP1` done. Dependency and licence audit ([`DEPENDENCIES.md`](DEPENDENCIES.md)) over 53 direct npm dependencies, 747 lockfile entries (191 production, 556 dev-only) and 19 vendored codebases, with 5 more bundled inside those. Findings: nothing shipped to a player is copyleft (the client bundle is MIT, Apache-2.0 and Zlib only); no GPL or AGPL at any depth, Valgrind being present only as its two BSD-licensed headers; exactly two MPL-2.0 items, `bitecs` 0.3.40 and the vendored CDT headers compiled into `picg.node` via `tri_split.cpp:6` and `pcg_geometry.cpp:5`, both unmodified and server-side; LGPL only in `@img/sharp-libvips-*` dev prebuilts. No dependency constrains which licence the engine can be released under, correcting the guess in [`ENGINE-CORE-SPLIT.md`](ENGINE-CORE-SPLIT.md) §5. Added [`../NOTICES.md`](../NOTICES.md) (generated by `scripts/gen-notices.mjs`, `--check` guards staleness) and `third_party/flatc/LICENSE` for the Apache-2.0 `flatc` binaries this public repo redistributes. Verified copyright lines against the in-tree files rather than memory, which corrected four of them. Follow-ups are `DEP2`: shipped client code imports `physx-js-webidl` from a `devDependency`.

- **2026-11-10**: PF4 prop-collision grid (#662, ✅): the SG4b grid's 32 m cells made each mover's query return nearly every static on the `prop-collision-tick` tile (500 props, 64 m), so the system cost 55.9 M instructions per tick against 18.3 M for the old scan. With 4 m cells it costs 5.9 M, and the walkers end every tick where the old scan put them (150,000 walker-ticks compared, 78,584 of them pushed by a prop). New workload `prop-collision-wide` (5,000 props, 200 m) is 8.8 M. `perf/callgrind-baseline.json` was measured on Node 22 and these numbers on Node 24, so it was not updated: it wants a re-baseline on one Node version in its own PR.

- **2026-11-03**: PF1 SectorIndex.frame leak (#650, ✅): old frames stayed reachable through `prior`, so heap grew about 26 MB/s at 200 players. Retained heap is flat after the fix and tick max falls from 356 and 383 ms to 145 and 72 ms at 200 players.
