- **2026-11-21**: Built the game client's UI style kit and rebuilt one screen on it (`🚧` `U15`, open PR `#737`). The client UI was the stock shadcn/Tailwind dark-slate admin theme (`--background: 222 47% 11%`, `rounded-lg` cards) with no visual system under it, which is why fantasy art kept reading as slop: a panel generated as one finished picture cannot tile, cannot resize without smearing its moulding, and brings its own light direction and palette. Added `apps/client/src/ui/kit/` — `tokens.css`, `kit.css`, `generate-textures.mjs` (nine tiling/9-slice textures from a seeded PRNG, zero dependencies, 44 KB), `check-contrast.mjs`, `preview.tsx`/`capture.mjs`, `README.md`. Two of its three rules come from the engine's cel shader (`D1`: banded diffuse, shadow colour off a ramp rather than multiplied to black), so every gradient is a hard-stop gradient and the UI shades by the world's rule rather than fighting it. `bag-panel-kit.tsx` rebuilds the bag/character panel (U10), importing `bag-panel.tsx`'s pure logic so behaviour cannot drift. The kit-sheet capture caught gain/loss at `1.11:1` on parchment (invisible); added `--kit-good-dark`/`--kit-bad-dark` applied inside `.kit-surface-parchment`/`.kit-tooltip` and covered both in the contrast check. `test/ui-kit.test.ts` asserts all 13 pairings and that the committed textures are byte-identical to the generator, so the PNGs cannot drift into hand-managed pictures. No licensed art involved — every texture is procedural. `@mmo-engine/client` `570/571` (`0` fail, `1` skipped), `npm run build` clean. Captures in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/client-ui/`. Evaluated FairyGUI first, as asked, and recommended against it: `fairygui-three` (MIT) renders correctly on three `r154`–`r185` and renders nothing on `r186`, the revision the client pins (silent, `0` draw calls vs `4`; its stage builds an orthographic camera with `near = 0` and `r186`'s frustum test culls every UI mesh). Last release 2024-07-08, editor is Windows/macOS only so authoring cannot run in Linux CI, and it draws UI as three.js meshes rather than DOM, which would cost React, i18n and the existing `aria-*` accessibility.
- **2026-11-26**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU4` complete — outliner component badges. Fourth time the gap list pointed at something we already had somewhere: the ECS entity list (`ui/ecs-inspector.tsx`) has badged every row with its components since it was written; the Scenes **Outliner**, which is the tree you actually build a scene in, drew only a name and a kind. `ui/outliner-badges.ts` holds the arithmetic (which chips fit, how many are left, how to shorten a compound name without losing which component it is) and `-view.tsx` the markup, split so the capture harness can mount the badges without pulling the whole Scenes panel and its hosts in behind them. A group node with no components gets nothing rather than a chip saying none, and the hover title names every component including the ones that did not fit. Markup stays in the Material 3 classes the Outliner already uses, since the forge theme restyles it through the token remap rather than a second component. 9 new tests (1,066 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail); capture in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/eu4-outliner-badges.png`.
- **2026-11-26**: Built `EB9`, what breaks before a module is disabled. The Modules panel let you flip a switch and find out at the next restart, though everything needed to say it beforehand was already in the panel's own state. `module-blast.ts` walks `dependsOn` transitively: the enabled modules that must go too (breadth-first, so the list reads the way the failure cascades), the systems leaving the tick order, the editor panels that disappear, the sidecars that stop. Every enabled card now carries the one line — "Turning this off takes 2 modules and 6 systems with it" — and a switch that takes other modules with it asks first, listing them, instead of acting. It also fixed a real bug rather than only reporting one: the panel closed the enabled set one level deep, so turning off `economy` dropped `items` but left `civic`, and the host refused the save with "requires items, which is not enabled" while the switch sprang back. `economy → items → civic` is this repository's own chain, which is what the capture shows. 15 new tests; captures `eb9-modules.png` and `eb9-blast-dialog.png`, the real panel against the real `/api/editor/modules` (the capture host refuses every write, so a picture of the repository cannot change it).
- **2026-11-26**: Built `EB2`, multi-select in the scene. Building a scene means moving five rocks together, lining up a row of torches and deleting a camp, and the editor could select exactly one node. The set and its click rules are pure (`scene-selection.ts`): a plain click replaces, ctrl or cmd toggles, shift ranges over the rows the outliner draws rather than over the tree's nesting, and the primary — the node the gizmo rides and Details shows — is the last one added, so "ctrl-click one more, then drag" does what it looks like. Deselecting the primary hands primary to what is left rather than dropping the gizmo. Align and distribute are arithmetic in `scene-align.ts` (align to the lowest, the middle of the spread or the highest; space evenly keeping the outermost two) and move only the nodes whose position actually changes, so no no-op undo step is recorded. A gizmo drag moves every selected node by the primary's delta inside one undo step, and Delete takes the whole set in one. The shared selection model still carries a single node, because that is what the Details pane is: the set belongs to the scene workspace. Box-select is deliberately not in — it needs a viewport drag that does not orbit, which is an input change of its own. 20 new tests; capture `eb2-multi-select.png` shows four nodes picked by ctrl-click with the align controls and "Delete 4".
- **2026-11-26**: Built `EB4` and `EB4b`, the viewport-first Scenes layout. The workspace was a form that happened to contain a viewport: the canvas sat between a wrapping toolbar above it and three rows of placeable chips below, capped at six rem, which is usable against a fixture scene and useless against the real roster of 25. The canvas is now the surface — the tools float on it in a translucent bar and the notes ("No terrain (grid only) · Stand-in sizes (no art pack)") sit in a status strip at its foot — and the placeables are a searchable, grouped, scrolling column beside the Outliner. Both it and the viewport read one hook (`ui/placeables-store.ts`), per the convention `EB11` wrote down, rather than fetching the list twice. `EB4b` turned out to be a layer deeper than the roadmap row assumed: the Scenes controls were not stock because of hardcoded classes but because `ui/components.tsx`'s primitives read the legacy shadcn aliases, which `forge.css` had never remapped — so every Button, Input and Select in the whole editor was Material 3 purple under the forge theme, not just this workspace. All twelve aliases are remapped, each in the channel space `app.css` consumes it in, and a test now fails if the two theme blocks ever disagree about which variables exist (checked by deleting one: it names the missing alias). 15 new tests; captures `eb4-scenes-workspace.png` and `eb4-palette-search.png`, both the real `ScenesPanel` against the host's own route handlers rather than fixtures.
- **2026-11-26**: Built `EB1`, the model-accurate scene viewport. Every node in the Scenes viewport was the same 1 m grey cube, so a wolf, a pine and a longhouse read identically and nothing in a scene could be judged by eye. The sizes are not the editor's invention: `scene-visuals-host.ts` reads the engine's own `CHARACTER_CAPSULE` and `SPECIES_NATURAL_SCALE` (now exported from `@mmo-engine/engine-core` rather than copied), applies the same "the creature's own `behavior.size` wins unless it is 1" rule `CreatureService.spawnOne` applies, and multiplies a prop's stand-in by `content/scale.json` the way the server does where baked objects spawn; `GET /api/editor/scene/visuals` serves the finished table with the art pack's model URL per id. The view loads a GLB once per URL and clones it per node at the scale the server spawns it with, never stretched to fit the stand-in, and falls back to the capsule or box when the pack has no model — which is every id in a checkout without `assets/`, where the toolbar now says "Stand-in sizes (no art pack)" instead of quietly lying. Wireframe keeps the stand-in on screen so the toggle still shows something. Drawing the real sizes immediately surfaced a content bug worth noting: `boar` is 1.8 m tall, because the roster's boar is not in `SPECIES_NATURAL_SCALE` and so spawns at a human's capsule. 17 new tests; captures `eb1-markers-before.png` and `eb1-sizes-after.png` in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/` are the same scene with the table off and on.
- **2026-11-26**: `EB11`, and section [1i](#1i-editor-build-out-the-tools-a-scene-building-editor-needs) opened. Walked the editor against what an Unreal/Unity-style editor needs — scene building, the login to character creation to in-game flow, the module system, and how the thing is layered — and the mockup walk's finding held a seventh time: almost everything is present as capability and the gaps are joins. The sharpest is `EB1`: the scene viewport draws a grey `BoxGeometry` per node while the editor already loads GLBs, resolves `AssetRef`s and draws terrain chunks. `EB11` is the one item that was not a join but an absence — the four layers (host, client, store, view) were real and unwritten, and had been broken by accident twice in one phase, which is why `playUiStore` and `getPlayController` had to be lifted out of `ui/play-mode.tsx`. [`EDITOR.md`](EDITOR.md) now names the layers and `apps/editor/test/layering.test.ts` holds five rules over the source text: a view may not call `fetch` (the seven panels that still do are listed by name, the list may only shrink, and a name that stops fetching fails the test so it cannot go stale), a store may not import a renderer, a host may not import React, a view may import a host's types but never its code, and a client may not reach more than its own route family. The guard was checked by breaking it: a `fetch` added to a panel not on the list fails the run and names the panel. 6 new tests.

- **2026-11-26**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU10` — the Blender live link, the last of the ten and the only tab in either mockup with nothing at all behind it. Its shape is decided by where the work happens: Blender pushes, the editor receives. [`scripts/blender/mmo_live_link.py`](../scripts/blender/mmo_live_link.py) is the add-on to copy, written against Blender's own Python and the standard library so there is nothing to `pip install` and nothing to keep in step with the engine's dependencies; it exports the selection to glTF and POSTs it, on a button or on every save of the `.blend`. The host streams that body into `assets/live/<name>.glb`. That folder is the point: `/assets/` is gitignored in full and `vcs-host.ts` refuses every path under it, so a model a designer is still iterating on cannot be committed by accident, and the live link writes nowhere else. This is the one route in the editor that writes binary from outside the browser, so it is deliberately narrow: the name is matched against a whitelist **before anything is opened** (which leaves no traversal, no hidden file and no device name to reason about), the first twelve bytes must be a `glTF` version 2 header, an empty export and one over 256 MB are both refused, and the body is streamed to a temporary file and renamed into place only once it is whole — a cancelled export leaves nothing for the editor to load. A re-push replaces the file and keeps one entry with its push count, and a `.glb` deleted by hand drops off the list on the next read. `ui/blender-panel.tsx` is the receiving end, showing the newest model through the editor's own `ModelPreview`; with nothing received it is the install instructions, because an empty panel that does not say how to fill itself is a dead end. **HTTP, not the websocket the mockup names**: a socket would only buy editor-to-Blender commands, and it would need a modal timer in Blender's main thread to pump, while the push direction needs neither. 22 new tests (1,148 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail), most of them on what the route refuses. Proof is a model that actually arrived: `src/ui/forge/capture-blender.mjs` pushes a committed test fixture through the real route — there is no Blender in a container, but everything after the POST is the shipped path — capture `eu10-blender-link.png`. Guide: [BLENDER-LINK.md](BLENDER-LINK.md).

- **2026-11-26**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU5` finished — the bottom dock regrouped. The styling reference folds the functionality reference's eight dock tabs into four, and that grouping is better, but it could not be built until `EU8` and `EU9` landed, because two of the four had nothing behind them and this shell does not draw a tab that says "soon". Under forge the dock is now **Console · Problems · Source Control · Integrations**; Material 3 keeps Output / Problems / Bakes, unchanged. Output and Bakes fold into the console, which already carries both streams, so the fold loses no output. Two departures from the reference, both deliberate and both recorded: **Problems stays**, because the reference has no validator over the content on disk, ours finds real breakage before a save, and dropping a working tab to match a picture is not a rebuild; and **Code does not become a tab**, because `EU9`'s answer to the Code tab is that the editor hands off to the person's own editor, and the handoff belongs where the locations are — on each system row — not behind a tab holding one button and a file tree we deliberately do not serve. The tab list is data in `ui/dock-tabs.ts` rather than markup in the shell, so a layout saved under one theme resolves to the other's counterpart instead of opening an empty pane (`output`/`bakes` ⇄ `console`), and a test asserts no id can ever resolve to a tab the dock is not showing. 11 new tests (1,126 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail). Proof is from the **running** editor this time, not a fixture: a new harness `src/ui/forge/capture-dock.mjs` drives the dev server and shoots each of the four tabs, failing the run on any page error, because a screenshot of a shell that threw looks like proof and is not. Captures `eu5-dock-{console,problems,source-control,integrations}.png`.

- **2026-11-26**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU9` — the VS Code handoff, which is the Code tab answered rather than built. An editor and a terminal inside the engine would undo two guards we hold on purpose: `files-host.ts` serves whitelisted document kinds only, and `vcs-host.ts` refuses every path under `assets/` because that art is licensed and confidential. What an embedded editor would have given us that is worth having is that **the editor already knows where things are** — every system node in the committed ECS graph carries `file:line` — so a click now opens that line in the editor the person already has. `code-handoff.ts` is the path arithmetic and the refusals, pure and browser-safe so the button and the host route cannot apply different rules: repository-relative paths only, no climbing out, and the same `assets/` refusal with the same trailing-dot normalisation `vcs-host.ts` does, because a tool that can open any file is the second tool that could cross that line. `code-host.ts` probes `code --version` once per host and spawns `code -g <file>:<line>` **detached and with an argv array**, so a path can hold any character without becoming a command, and a file this checkout does not have is refused rather than opened as an empty buffer. Three routes to the same place, tried in order and each unavailable for its own reason: the host spawn (the only one that can say *why* it failed), the `vscode://` URI (no host needed, only the scheme registered), and `code -g …` on the clipboard — the last being the only one left when the person's editor is not VS Code at all. The Integrations row for VS Code is now live off the probe: found reports the version, missing reports *not set up* rather than blocked, because the other two routes still work. 34 new tests (1,115 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail), most of them on the refusals; captures in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/eu4-systems.png` (the chip on every row) and `eu5-integrations.png`.

- **2026-11-26**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU8` (part) — the console, which is what unblocks the four-tab dock. The streams all existed and the console did not: the play server's log was polled into the Server tab, bakes went to the Bakes tab, and the editor's own notifications flashed in a snackbar for 3.6 seconds and were then gone for good — the worst of the three, because a line you cannot scroll back to may as well not have been shown. `ui/console-log.ts` is one bounded ring of 1,000 entries that all three write to and `ui/forge-console.tsx` reads, with filters by source, level and substring, and per-level counts on the chips. **It polls nothing**: whoever already polls the server log pushes its page in (`dock-panes.tsx`), `notify()` mirrors every notification, and one subscription mirrors the bakes store, installed once from `main.tsx` so a hot reload cannot double every bake line. Server lines take their level from the same `lineTone` the Server tab colours by — one rule, not two — and blank log padding is dropped rather than logged as empty rows. An empty console says *which* kind of empty it is, because "nothing logged yet" and "your filter hides everything" are different problems with different fixes. 14 new tests (1,080 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail); capture in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/eu8-console.png`. Runtime discovery, the other half of `EU8`, is still open.

- **2026-11-26**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU5` (Integrations) — `ui/forge-integrations.tsx`. The styling reference folds the functionality reference's eight dock tabs into four, of which Integrations is a status list. **The regrouping itself cannot be built honestly yet**: Source Control already exists behind the Changes workspace, but Code and Console have nothing behind them, and drawing two empty tabs is exactly what the shell's own "no 'soon' placeholders" rule forbids — so the status list is the part that ships now, over connections that already exist: the play server, the git worktree, the advisory soft locks in `content/locks.json` (the reference's "Conflict Guard", already ours under a duller name), and `checkGraphStaleness` on the committed ECS graph. The two we have not built, the VS Code handoff (`EU9`) and the Blender live-link (`EU10`), are listed as *not set up* with the slice that would build each: a status list that hides its holes is not a status list. Each read fails on its own, so one missing route cannot blank the rest, and an unreachable read is reported as unreachable rather than as healthy — a test asserts no row can claim to be connected on no data. 11 new tests (1,057 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail); capture in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/eu5-integrations.png`.
- **2026-11-26**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU4` (systems half) — `ui/forge-systems.tsx`. The mockup lists every system with a component signature and a cost beside it; both already existed here, in two places that had never met. The committed ECS graph indexes access per COMPONENT, so the panel inverts it into one row per system (a written component outranks the same component read, since that is what decides ordering and conflicts) and reports each row at its WEAKEST confidence, because a system whose access was only inferred from its source may have an incomplete signature. Costs come from the profiler's shared `useTickTimings()`, joined by name: a cost with no matching system invents no row, and a system the server never reported simply has no number — with no server running the panel says so instead of printing zeros. Over the real committed graph that is 38 systems, 27 of which declare their access. 13 new tests (1,046 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail); capture in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/eu4-systems.png`, shot over `content/graph/ecs-graph.json` itself. Current `main` merged in (`#749`, `#750`, `#756`, `#757` and the client HUD work) before testing; `#751` is out of draft. Outliner badges remain open in `EU4`.
- **2026-11-22**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU7` (part) — the profiler. Third time the gap list was wrong in the same direction: "profiler / per-system tick cost" was listed as needing a server feed, and the feed has existed since the gateway was written. `TickTimings` records every system's cost inside `runTick`, the debug API answers `query: "timings"`, and the Debug workspace has shown the top five as a plain list. What was missing is the budget — 0.8 ms means nothing until you know the tick has 33.33 ms and that 0.8 ms is 2.4% of it. `ui/forge-profiler.tsx` draws every system as a share of the tick with p50/p99 against the budget and the headroom left at p99, and the poll moved into a shared `useTickTimings()` that BOTH presentations mount, so they cannot drift on what they show or how often they ask. `getPlayController` moved out of `ui/play-mode.tsx` into `ui/play-controller-slot.ts` for the same reason `playUiStore` did: asking the running server a question should not drag `@mmo-engine/client/render` in behind it. 11 new tests (1,033 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail); capture in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/eu7-profiler.png`. **Found while building it, not fixed here** (`PERF-TT1` below): the snapshot mixes two windows — `p50`/`p99` come from a 1,000-tick ring, but each system's `avg` and `max` are running totals since the process started, so a spike never decays out of `max` and a regression starting now is diluted by every tick before it, while the type's own comment calls `max` "the window". The panel labels which number is which rather than implying one window. The fix is a change to the tick path and belongs in its own PR behind `valgrind:perf`.
- **2026-11-22**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU6` (part) — viewport gizmos and the corner axis triad. The gap list had "viewport gizmos" as missing; they were not. `ui/scene-view.ts` has had full `TransformControls` since EA5. What was missing is that the mode lived in `scene-viewport.tsx`'s own `useState` with its own W/E/R keys, disconnected from the shared tool store the toolbar and every other viewport read — so the toolbar could say *move* while the viewport showed a rotate ring, and the new context bar would report the toolbar's answer. `viewport-tools.ts` already said "rotate and scale are reserved for the scene viewport's gizmos", so one store was always the intent: `gizmoModeFor` / `toolForGizmoMode` are now that mapping, the viewport reads `useTool()`, its mode buttons write the shared tool, and `select` hides the gizmo entirely — camera and pick, which is what `select` already means everywhere else. A Select button joins the row with `V`. The corner triad is three's own `ViewHelper`, rendered after the main pass with the depth buffer cleared and clickable to snap the camera to an axis (a click it takes is not also a pick); it is a viewport flag like grid and gizmos, so the View menu toggles it and a viewport without one disables the item with a reason. 5 new tests (1,022 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail). Still open in `EU6`: the Scene / Game / Prefab mode segment.
- **2026-11-22**: Editor UI 2026 slice `EU2` built — the forge context bar (`apps/editor/src/ui/forge-chrome.tsx`). The functionality reference puts one strip under the toolbar saying where you are and what the project is doing; today that is scattered across a workspace tab, a view tab, whichever inspector owns the selection, the Changes workspace and a colour on the toolbar border. The bar gathers them and invents nothing: the crumbs come from the shell's own router state and `editorSelection`, the tool chip from `tool-store.ts` (the store every viewport gates its edit gesture on), the server chip from `playUiStore` in the controller's own state vocabulary, the branch chip from `vcsStatus()` — the same `/api/editor/vcs/status` the Source Control panel calls — with a conflict outranking a plain change so the one tone it shows is the worst thing in the tree, and the problem count from the shell's own total. It mounts only under `[data-theme="forge"]`, since there is no Material 3 counterpart to restyle. Two supporting changes, both improvements in their own right: `createStore().useValue` and `useEditorSelection()` now pass a server snapshot to `useSyncExternalStore`, without which React refuses to render any store-reading component to static markup (so no such panel could be tested); and `playUiStore` moved out of `ui/play-mode.tsx` into `ui/play-ui-store.ts`, because reading the play state used to drag `@mmo-engine/client/render` and `/debug` in behind it — `play-mode.tsx` re-exports it, so no importer changed. 12 new tests (1,017 pass in `apps/editor`, 0 fail), ECS graph byte-identical (`hash=6585d271f989`). Still and GIF in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026/`, shot by `ui/forge/capture-shell.mjs`, whose every GIF frame is the real component reacting to a real store write.
- **2026-11-22**: Editor UI 2026 planned and its first two slices built (`EU1`, `EU3` 🚧, [`EDITOR-UI-2026.md`](EDITOR-UI-2026.md)). Walked both mockups the owner sent, screen by screen, and mapped them onto today's editor: the functionality reference's eight-tab dock and floating selection card are largely capability we already have drawn differently (`vcs-host.ts` already does status/diff/log/stage/commit/pull/push **and** the advisory file locks its "Conflict Guard" shows; `/api/editor/graph` already indexes per-system component access; the authoring view already summons floating widgets on selection), while a code editor, a terminal, a Blender live-link, a profiler and per-system tick cost have nothing behind them. Measured the styling reference's rendered DOM into a token system (grounds `#05080E`/`#050A12`, hairline `#263646`, one cyan accent `#22D3EE` plus three semantic hues, radii 0/2/4, bars 56/36/28, Rajdhani-Inter-JetBrains Mono at 8-11px) and shipped it as `apps/editor/src/forge.css`, which remaps the existing `--m3-*` roles so every panel restyles at once — the editor reads those tokens 453 times and holds only 126 hardcoded hex values across 21 files. `EU3` rebuilds the entity inspector (`ui/forge-inspector.tsx`) over the SAME `useEcsInspectorSession()` hook the Material 3 panel mounts, extracted from `ui/ecs-inspector.tsx` for the purpose, so the two cannot drift; `ui/forge/preview.tsx` + `capture.mjs` photograph both over one fixture snapshot with the editor's real Tailwind output. Rulings recorded in the note: the mockups' UI SDK tab is the **client's** kit, not the editor's, so it is dropped here (`U15` keeps it); FairyGUI is still rejected as a runtime, though its controller/gear pattern is worth copying in plain React; and an embedded IDE plus terminal would undo the path guards in `files-host.ts` and the `assets/` refusal in `vcs-host.ts`, so `EU9` is a VS Code handoff instead. Theme is opt-in (`?theme=forge`, `src/theme.ts`); Material 3 stays the default. Captures in `/mnt/project-files/marketing/editor-ui-2026-11-22/`.
